15: function roundTextHighlight(ctx: Ctx, x: number, y: number, w: number, h: number, r: number): void {
16:   const rad = Math.min(r, w / 2, h / 2)

18:   ctx.moveTo(x + rad, y)
19:   ctx.lineTo(x + w - rad, y)

25:   ctx.lineTo(x, y + rad)

27:   ctx.closePath()
28: }

32: function primaryFontFamily(family: string): string {
33:   const first = family.split(',')[0]?.trim() ?? family
34:   return first.replace(/^['"]|['"]$/g, '')
35: }
36: 
37: function buildFont(
38:   size: number,
39:   weight: number = 400,
40:   family: string = 'Inter',
41:   italic: boolean = false
42: ): string {
43:   const primary = primaryFontFamily(family)
44:   return `${italic ? 'italic ' : ''}${weight} ${size}px "${primary}"`
45: }
46: 
47: function applyGradientFill(
48:   ctx: Ctx,
49:   gradient: VeloxGradient,
50:   x: number, y: number,
51:   width: number, height: number
52: ): void {
53:   const angle = (parseFloat(gradient.angle ?? '0') * Math.PI) / 180
54:   const len = Math.sqrt(width * width + height * height)
55:   const cx = x + width / 2
56:   const cy = y + height / 2
57:   const gx1 = cx - (Math.cos(angle) * len) / 2
58:   const gy1 = cy - (Math.sin(angle) * len) / 2
59: 
60:   const grad = ctx.createLinearGradient(gx1, gy1, cx + (Math.cos(angle) * len) / 2, cy + (Math.sin(angle) * len) / 2)
61:   if (gradient.stops.length === 0) {
62:     ctx.fillStyle = 'transparent'
63:     return
64:   }
65:   if (gradient.stops.length === 1) {
66:     grad.addColorStop(0, gradient.stops[0])
67:     grad.addColorStop(1, gradient.stops[0])
68:   } else {
69:     gradient.stops.forEach((stop, i) => {
70:       grad.addColorStop(i / (gradient.stops.length - 1), stop)
71:     })
72:   }
73:   ctx.fillStyle = grad
74: }
75:

78: /**
79:  * Break `text` into lines that each fit within `maxWidth` pixels.
80:  * Respects explicit `\n` line breaks in the source string.
81:  */
82: function wrapLines(ctx: Ctx, text: string, maxWidth: number): string[] {
83:   const result: string[] = []
84: 
85:   for (const paragraph of text.split('\n')) {
86:     const words = paragraph.split(' ')
87:     let line = ''
88: 
89:     for (const word of words) {
90:       const candidate = line ? `${line} ${word}` : word
91:       if (ctx.measureText(candidate).width <= maxWidth) {
92:         line = candidate
93:       } else {
94:         if (line) result.push(line)
95:         // If a single word is wider than maxWidth, push it as-is (unavoidable)
96:         line = word
97:       }
98:     }
99:     if (line) result.push(line)
100:   }
101: 
102:   return result.length ? result : ['']
103: }
104:

151:     if (style === 'karaoke' || style === 'highlightKeywords') {

268:         const clipX = anchorX + (textAlign === 'right' ? -w : textAlign === 'center' ? -w / 2 : 0)
269:         c.rect(clipX, lineY - fontSize, clipW, fontSize * 2)
270:         c.clip()
271:       }
272: 
273:       // Gradient fill on text
274:       if (gradient) {
275:         const measured = c.measureText(line)
276:         const w = measured.width + letterSpacing * Math.max(0, line.length - 1)
277:         applyGradientFill(c, gradient, -w / 2, lineY - fontSize / 2, w, fontSize)
278:       } else {
279:         c.fillStyle = color
280:       }
281: 
282:       // Draw with letter spacing
283:       if (letterSpacing !== 0) {
284:         let cx = 0
285:         c.save()
286:         if (textAlign === 'center') {
287:           const total = line.split('').reduce((acc, ch) => acc + c.measureText(ch).width + letterSpacing, 0)
288:           // Subtract the extra trailing letterSpacing
289:           cx = -(total - letterSpacing) / 2
290:           c.textAlign = 'left'
291:         }
292:         for (const ch of line) {
293:           c.fillText(ch, cx + anchorX, lineY)
294:           cx += c.measureText(ch).width + letterSpacing
295:         }
296:         c.restore()
297:       } else {
298:         c.fillText(line, anchorX, lineY)
299:       }
300: 
301:       if (state.clipReveal < 1) c.restore()
302:     })
303: 
304:     if (maxHeight) c.restore()
305:     if (state.clipRevealY !== undefined && state.clipRevealY < 1) c.restore()
306: 
307:     c.restore()
308:   }
309: 
310:   if (state.blur > 0 && !supportsCanvasFilter) {
311:     applyElementBlur(ctx, drawX + state.x, drawY + state.y, maxWidth / 2, totalHeight / 2, state.blur, drawInto)
312:   } else {
313:     if (supportsCanvasFilter && state.blur > 0) {
314:       ctx.save()
315:       setCanvasFilter(ctx, `blur(${state.blur}px)`)
316:     }
317:     drawInto(ctx)
318:     if (supportsCanvasFilter && state.blur > 0) ctx.restore()
319:   }
320: }
321: 
322: // ─── Text List Draw ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
323: 
324: export function drawTextList(
325:   ctx: Ctx,
326:   el: TextListElementConfig,
327:   drawX: number,
328:   drawY: number,
329:   localFrame: number,
330:   fps: number,
331:   canvasWidth: number,
332:   canvasHeight: number
333: ): void {
334:   const {
335:     items,
336:     fontSize = 28,
337:     fontWeight = 400,
338:     fontFamily = 'Inter',
339:     color = '#ffffff',
340:     gap = 20,
341:     bullet = '•',
342:     staggerAnimation,
343:     staggerInterval = 0.15,
344:   } = el
345: 
346:   // Default max width: remaining space from this element's center to the right safe margin.
347:   const maxWidth = el.maxWidth ?? Math.max(120, Math.round(canvasWidth * 0.94) - drawX)
348: 
349:   ctx.font = buildFont(fontSize, fontWeight, fontFamily)
350:   ctx.textBaseline = 'middle'
351: 
352:   let cursorY = drawY
353: 
354:   items.forEach((item, i) => {
355:     const itemDelay = i * staggerInterval * fps
356:     let opacity = 1
357:     let offsetY = 0
358: 
359:     if (staggerAnimation) {
360:       const progress = Math.max(0, Math.min(1, (localFrame - itemDelay) / (0.3 * fps)))
361:       if (progress <= 0) { opacity = 0 }
362:       else if (progress < 1) {
363:         opacity = progress
364:         if (staggerAnimation === 'slideUp') offsetY = (1 - progress) * 20
365:       }
366:     }
367: 
368:     const prefix = bullet ? `${bullet} ` : ''
369:     const prefixWidth = ctx.measureText(prefix).width
370:     const textMaxWidth = Math.max(maxWidth - prefixWidth, 100)
371: 
372:     // Word-wrap each list item
373:     const wrappedLines = wrapLines(ctx, item, textMaxWidth)
374:     const lineH = fontSize * 1.3
375: 
376:     ctx.save()
377:     ctx.globalAlpha = opacity
378:     ctx.translate(0, offsetY)
379:     ctx.fillStyle = color
380:     ctx.textAlign = 'left'
381: 
382:     wrappedLines.forEach((line, li) => {
383:       const y = cursorY + li * lineH
384:       ctx.fillText((li === 0 ? prefix : '  ') + line, drawX, y)
385:     })
386: 
387:     ctx.restore()
388: 
389:     cursorY += wrappedLines.length * lineH + gap
390:   })
391: }

1591: 128: 
1592: 129:   const { canvas: scratch, ctx: sctx } = getScratch(w, h)
1593: 130:   sctx.setTransform(1, 0, 0, 1, 0, 0)
1594: 131:   sctx.clearRect(0, 0, w, h)
1595: 132:   sctx.save()
1596: 133:   sctx.translate(-x, -y)
1597: 134:   if (blurScale < 1) sctx.scale(blurScale, blurScale)
1598: 135:   drawFn(sctx)
1599: 136:   sctx.restore()
1600: 137: 
1601: 138:   const imageData = sctx.getImageData(0, 0, w, h)
1602: 139:   if (blurRadius >= 0.35) blurImageDataRGBA(imageData.data, w, h, blurRadius * blurScale)
1603: 140:   if (postProcess) postProcess(imageData)
1604: 141:   sctx.putImageData(imageData, 0, 0)
1605: 142: 
1606: 143:   targetCtx.save()
1607: 144:   targetCtx.imageSmoothingEnabled = true
1608: 145:   targetCtx.imageSmoothingQuality = 'high'
1609: 146:   targetCtx.drawImage(scratch as unknown as CanvasImageSource, x, y, fullW, fullH)
1610: 147:   targetCtx.restore()
1611: 148: }
1612: 149: 
1613: 150: /** In-place brightness (multiply) + saturation (lerp toward luma) on RGBA data. */
1614: 151: export function adjustBrightnessSaturation(
1615: 152:   data: Uint8ClampedArray,
1616: 153:   brightness = 1,
1617: 154:   saturate = 1,
1618: 155: ): void {
1619: 156:   for (let i = 0; i < data.length; i += 4) {
1620: 157:     let r = data[i] * brightness
1621: 158:     let g = data[i + 1] * brightness
1622: 159:     let b = data[i + 2] * brightness
1623: 160:     if (saturate !== 1) {
1624: 161:       const l = 0.299 * r + 0.587 * g + 0.114 * b
1625: 162:       r = l + (r - l) * saturate
1626: 163:       g = l + (g - l) * saturate
1627: 164:       b = l + (b - l) * saturate
1628: 165:     }
1629: 166:     data[i] = r
1630: 167:     data[i + 1] = g
1631: 168:     data[i + 2] = b
1632: 169:   }
1633: 170: }
1634: 
1635: (End of file - total 170 lines)
1636: </content>

1978: <content>
1979: 1: # @velox-video/core
1980: 2: 
1981: 3: LLM-friendly motion graphics primitives for deterministic Canvas video rendering.
1982: 4: 
1983: 5: Velox Core powers the `velox-video` CLI and playground. It defines videos as serializable TypeScript configs, renders with Canvas in browser/Node, and exposes a VML compiler so small LLMs can produce reliable motion graphics without inventing brittle method chains.
1984: 6: 
1985: 7: It also now includes a broader production surface for reel-style content: reusable themes, transition-aware scenes, local placeholder media resolution, and lintable markup so generated videos can be checked before rendering.
1986: 8: 
1987: 9: ## Install
1988: 10: 
1989: 11: ```bash
1990: 12: npm install @velox-video/core
1991: 13: ```
1992: 14: 
1993: 15: For rendering and previewing from the command line, install the CLI:
1994: 16: 
1995: 17: ```bash
1996: 18: npm install -D velox-video
1997: 19: ```
1998: 20: 
1999: 21: ## VML First
2000: 22: 
2001: 23: VML is the recommended LLM format:
2002: 24: 
2003: 25: ```ts
2004: 26: import { createVideoFromMarkup } from '@velox-video/core'
2005: 27: 
2006: 28: export default createVideoFromMarkup(`
2007: 29: <video size="portrait" fps="60" theme="creamChecks" background="creamGrid" motionQuality="premium">
2008: 30:   <scene duration="4.8" template="topTextBottomVisual" camera="slowPush" mood="editorial" transition="blurDissolve">
2009: 31:     <announcement slot="top" title="Launch reels from markup" subtitle="Templates, captions, assets, cards, and beats." badge="NEW" motion="heroCinematic" />
2010: 32:     <asset slot="visual" name="phone-frame" width="360" height="640" motion="driftIn" />
2011: 33:     <captions slot="caption" text="Launch reels from markup with safe VML." style="karaoke" />
2012: 34:   </scene>
2013: 35:   <scene duration="5.5" template="headlineThenProof" background="warmPaper" camera="slowPush" mood="editorial">
2014: 36:     <breakingNews slot="top" headline="No-key media starts free-first" ticker="Generated fallback, local, Wikipedia, Unsplash Source, OpenBrand metadata." tone="warning" />
2015: 37:     <githubRepo slot="visual" owner="sanjaymalladi" repo="velox" motion="driftIn" />
2016: 38:     <icon slot="overlay" name="github" size="104" motion="magneticPop" />
2017: 39:   </scene>
2018: 40: </video>
2019: 41: `)
2020: 42: ```
2021: 43: 
2022: 44: ## Reel VML Surface
2023: 45: 
2024: 46: Core now includes a reel production layer designed for AI-generated shorts:
2025: 47: 
2026: 48: - Scene templates and slots: `template="topTextBottomVisual"` plus `slot="top|visual|caption|overlay|left|right"`.
2027: 49: - Semantic components: `<announcement>`, `<launchCard>`, `<breakingNews>`, `<featureReveal>`, `<problemSolution>`, `<beforeAfter>`, `<quoteCard>`, `<ranking>`, `<countdown>`, `<finalCTA>`.
2028: 50: - Captions: `<captions text="..." style="karaoke|pill|wordPop|highlightKeywords" />` and timed `<caption at="..." dur="...">...</caption>`.
2029: 51: - Built-in transparent SVG assets/icons: `<asset name="phone-frame" />`, `<asset name="new-badge" />`, `<icon name="github" />`.
2030: 52: - Media placeholders for CLI preprocessing: `<stock provider="wikipedia" query="..." />`, `<githubRepo />`, `<npmPackage />`, `<brandCard />`, `<website />`.
2031: 53: - Audio timeline metadata: root `music`, `<audio>`, `<sfx>`, and `<beat>` compile to `audioPlan` for future muxing; current MP4 export is silent.
2032: 54: 
2033: 55: ## Themes And Transitions
2034: 56: 
2035: 57: Velox ships with a theme catalog and transition presets that can be used from both markup and the TypeScript API:
2036: 58: 
2037: 59: - Theme selection with `theme="..."` and programmatic theme helpers from `themes`.
2038: 60: - Scene transitions such as `blurDissolve` and other reusable motion-aware scene handoffs.
2039: 61: - Theme previews and generated docs pages are built from the same theme metadata used by the runtime.
2040: 62: 
2041: 63: ## Markup Checks
2042: 64: 
2043: 65: The core package includes linting support for markup and video configs so invalid scenes can be caught earlier in the workflow.
2044: 66: 
2045: 67: ```ts
2046: 68: import { lintVeloxMarkup } from '@velox-video/core'
2047: 69: 
2048: 70: const result = lintVeloxMarkup(`<video><scene duration="4"/></video>`)
2049: 71: ```
2050: 72: 
2051: 73: Use this before rendering generated `.vml` files or template output.
2052: 74: 
2053: 75: ## TypeScript API
2054: 76: 
2055: 77: ```ts
2056: 78: import { createVideo, scene, text, shape, backdrops, motion } from '@velox-video/core'
2057: 79: 
2058: 80: export default createVideo({
2059: 81:   size: 'portrait',
2060: 82:   fps: 60,
2061: 83:   theme: 'obsidian',
2062: 84:   motionQuality: 'premium',
2063: 85:   background: backdrops.grid('rgba(255,255,255,0.05)', 44),
2064: 86:   scenes: [
2065: 87:     scene(5)
2066: 88:       .camera('slowPush')
2067: 89:       .mood('cinematic')

2083: 105: 
2084: 106: ## Highlights
2085: 107: 
2086: 108: - Deterministic Canvas rendering for browser preview and native export.
2087: 109: - VML compiler for LLM-safe video generation.
2088: 110: - Scene-level camera controls: `slowPush`, `parallaxDrift`, `handheld`, `kenBurns`.
2089: 111: - Premium motion presets powered by Popmotion easing.
2090: 112: - D3-backed charts: bar, line, and donut charts.
2091: 113: - Flubber-backed morph blobs for organic motion accents.
2092: 114: - Color helpers powered by Culori and Chroma.
2093: 115: - Bundled SVGL logo path rendering through `@velox-video/svgl`.
2094: 116: - Reel templates, captions, semantic components, local transparent SVG assets, generated cards, and stock/media placeholder refs.
2095: 117: 
2096: 118: ## Public Exports
2097: 119: 
2098: 120: Common exports include:
2099: 121: 
2100: 122: - `createVideo`, `scene`
2101: 123: - `text`, `shape`, `image`, `logo`, `group`, `layout`
2102: 124: - `backdrops`, `typography`, `creativeCards`, `motion`, `colors`, `themes`
2103: 125: - `createVideoFromMarkup`, `isVeloxMarkup`, `lintVeloxMarkup`
2104: 126: - `drawFrame`, `getTotalFrames`, `preloadImages`, `setImageCache`
2105: 127: - `encodeVeloxStockRef`, `encodeVeloxCardRef`, `decodeVeloxStockRef`, `decodeVeloxCardRef`
2106: 128: - `parseSrt`, `splitWords`, `buildCaptionWordSpans`
2107: 129: 
2108: 130: ## License
2109: 131: 
2110: 132: MIT
2111:

2258: Based on the codebase (VML compiler, scene templates, captions, Node `@napi-rs/canvas` export, D3 charts, popmotion easing, silent MP4 export, theme catalog), here's a prioritized feature list to push it to premium:
2259: 
2260: ## 🔊 Audio & Voice (biggest gap — export is currently silent)
2261: - **Real audio muxing** — pipe the existing `audioPlan` (music/SFX/voice) into the MP4 via ffmpeg/ffmpeg.wasm instead of discarding it.

2264: - **Duck/mix engine** — auto-duck music under voiceover, beat-synced SFX.
2265: 
2266: ## 🎬 Rendering & Export
2267: - **More formats** — transparent WebM, GIF, PNG/image-sequence, HDR, plus auto aspect-ratio variants (1:1, 16:9, 9:16) from one source.
2268: - **GPU/WebGL backend** — optional Three.js/WebGPU renderer for shaders & particles beyond 2D Canvas.
2269: - **Distributed/cloud render** — Lambda render farm + resumable jobs (the hyperframes skill already references this pattern).
2270: - **Faster blur** — replace `stackblur-canvas` CPU path with WASM/SIMD or GPU for 4K.
2271: 
2272: ## ✨ Effects & Visual Quality
2273: - **Post-processing stack** — bloom/glow, drop shadows, chromatic aberration, vignette, film grain, lens flare, glitch/CRT, pixel-sort.
2274: - **Rich gradient & material system** — mesh gradients, noise fields, glassmorphism, neon, metallic.
2275: - **Particle systems** — emitters, physics, trails (beyond the current static `particles` shape).
2276: 
2277: ## 🎞 Motion & Animation
2278: - **Kinetic typography** — per-character/word stagger, text-on-path, 3D text, rubber-band/elastic springs.
2279: - **Path & rig animation** — animate elements along bézier paths, follow/magnetic motion.
2280: - **Lottie import/export** — render Lottie JSON, and export Velox scenes to Lottie.
2281: - **Animated data viz** — live-updating charts, count-up numbers, progress bars feeding from JSON/CSV.
2282: - **More transitions** — 3D flip, page-curl, morph, light-leak, mask wipes beyond `blurDissolve`.
2283:

2472: - **Lightweight** — zero runtime dependencies, tree-shakeable ESM/CJS dual output
2473: - **Resilient parsing** — tolerates malformed ASS files (wrong Format lines, missing PlayRes, case-insensitive sections, BOM, flexible timestamps)
2474: ...

2477: | SRT | Yes | Yes |

2479: | ASS/SSA | Yes | Yes (advanced effects) |
2480: | SBV | Yes | Yes |
2481: | TTML/DFXP | Yes | Yes |

2568: Highlights:
2569: Displays subtitles in .ass format from JavaScript. Supports most SSA/ASS features, easily integrates with HTML5 videos.
2570: ...
2571: SubtitlesOctopus displays subtitles in .ass format and easily integrates with HTML5 videos.
2572: Since it uses [libass](https://github.com/libass/libass), SubtitlesOctopus supports most
2573: SSA/ASS features and enables you to get consistent results in authoring and web-playback,
2574: provided libass is also used locally.
2575: ...
2576: - Supports most SSA/ASS features (everything libass supports)
2577: - Supports all OpenType- and TrueType-fonts (including woff2 fonts)
2578: - Works fast (because uses WebAssembly with fallback to asm.js if it's not available)
2579: - Uses Web Workers thus video and interface doesn't lag even on "heavy" subtitles (working in background)
2580: - Doesn't use DOM manipulations and render subtitles on single canvas
2581: - Fully compatible with [libass'](https://github.com/libass/libass) extensions
2582:  (but beware of compatability to other ASS-renderers when using them)
2583: - Easy to use - just connect it to video element
2584: ...
2585: ### Using only with canvas
2586: ...
2587: You're also able to use it without any video. However, that requires you to set
2588: the time the subtitles should render at yourself:
2589: ...
2590: var options = {
2591:     canvas: document.getElementById('canvas'),
2592: ...
2593: subUrl: '/test/test.ass', // Link to subtitles
2594: ...
2595: fonts: ['/test/font-1.ttf', '/test/font-2.ttf'],
2596: ...
2597: Links to fonts
2598: ...
2599: default font already
2600: ...
2601: workerUrl
2602: ...
2603: js-worker.
2604: ...
2605: instance.setCurrentTime(15); // Render subtitles at 00:15 on your canvas
2606: ...
2607: - `canvas`: The canvas to render the subtitles to. If none is given it will
2608:  create a new canvas and insert it as a sibling of the video element (only if
2609:  the video element exists). (Optional)
2610: ...
2611: - `renderMode`: Rendering mode.
2612:  (If not set, the deprecated option `lossyRender` is evaluated)
2613:  - `js-blend` - JS Blending
2614:  - `wasm-blend` - WASM Blending, currently the default
2615:  - `lossy` - Lossy Render Mode (EXPERIMENTAL)
2616: ...
2617: - `prescaleFactor
2618: ...
2619: Scale down (`
2620: ...
2621: 1.0`) the
2622: ...

2624:  performance at the expense of quality,
2625: ...

2634: ...
2635: ### Rendering Modes
2636: ...
2637: upon instance creation
2638: ...
2639: outside of WebAssembly.
2640: ...
2641: #### WASM Blending
2642: ...
2643: To use this mode set `renderMode` to `wasm-blend` upon instance creation.
2644: This will blend the bitmaps of the different events together in WebAssembly,
2645: so the JavaScript-part only needs to process a single image.
2646: If WebAssembly-support is available this will be faster than the default mode,
2647: especially for many and/or complex simultaneous subtitles.
2648: Without WebAssembly-support it will fallback to asm.js and
2649: should at least not be slower than the default mode.
2650: ...
2651: To use this mode set `renderMode` to `lossy` upon instance creation.
2652: ...
2653: The Lossy Render mode has been created by @no1d as a suggestion for fix browser
2654: freezing when rendering heavy subtitles (#46), it uses
2655: [createImageBitmap](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/WindowOrWorkerGlobalScope/createImageBitmap)
2656: to render the bitmap in the Worker, using Promises instead of direct render on
2657: canvas in the Main Thread. When the browser start to hang, it will not lock main
2658: thread, instead will run Async, so if the function createImageBitmap fail, it
2659: will not stop the rendering process at all and may cause some bitmap loss or
2660: simply will not draw anything in canvas, mostly on low end devices.

2664: Title: luxluth/ass-html5

2669: Display ASS/SSA subtitles for html video
2670: ...
2671: - aegisub

2673: - html-canvas
2674: - html5
2675: - javascript

2680: ...
2681: Display ASS/SSA subtitles on html5 videos
2682: ...
2683: - **Karaoke Support**: Full support for `\k`, `\kf`, `\ko`, and `\K` tags.
2684: - **Vector Drawings**: High-performance rendering of ASS vector paths (`\p`) using `Path2D`.
2685: - **Clipping**: Support for rectangular and vector clipping/masking (`\clip`, `\iclip`).
2686: - **Advanced Transformations**:
2687:  - Rotation around all axes (`\frz`, `\frx`, `\fry`).
2688:  - Scaling (`\fscx`, `\fscy`) and Shearing (`\fax`, `\fay`).
2689:  - Custom rotation origin (`\org`).
2690: ...
2691: - **Smart Wrapping**: Implementation of `\q` styles for optimal line breaking.
2692: - **Collision Handling**: Automatic vertical shifting to prevent overlapping subtitles.
2693: - **Blur Effects**: Edge blur (`\be`) and Gaussian blur (`\blur`) using Canvas filters.
2694: - **Performance Optimized**: Built-in layer caching to minimize redundant redraws.
2695: ...
2696: | option | description | required | type | default |
2697: | :-----: | :---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------: | :------: | :---------------------------: | :-------------------: |
2698: | assText | The ass text string | 🚫 | `string` | `undefined` |
2699: | subUrl | URL to fetch the ASS content from | 🚫 | `string` | `undefined` |
2700: | video | The video to display the subtile on. Can be either an `HTMLVideoElement` or `string` (html query selector ) | ✅ | `HTMLVideoElement` / `string` | `undefined` |
2701: | fonts | Custom fonts to load | 🚫 | [`Font[]`](src/ass.ts#L78) | `undefined` |
2702: | zIndex | zIndex of the rendering frame | 🚫 | `number` | Drawn after the video |
2703: | onReady | A Callback that is invoked when the preprocess of the ass text by render is done | 🚫 | `() => void` | `undefined` |
2704: | logging | Type of logging | 🚫 | [`LOGTYPE`](src/ass.ts#L50) | `LOGTYPE.DISABLE` |
2705: ...
2706: > One of `assText` or `subUrl` is required.
2707: ...
2708: > [!NOTE]
2709: > The simple `video` tag element, in fullscreen mode, the position of the video is absolutely on top of any element.
2710: > No other element can go on top of it.
2711: >
2712: > It's therefore recommended to use a third party player rather than the native one. You can see an example with [plry](https://github.com/sampotts/plyr) [here](#svelte-and-plry).

2813: Title: ThaUnknown/jassub
2814: URL: https://github.com/ThaUnknown/jassub/
2815: Published: N/A
2816: Author: N/A
2817: Highlights:
2818: Displays subtitles in .ass format via JavaScript. Supports all SSA/ASS features, easily integrates with HTML5 videos.
2819: ...
2820: ## README
2821: 
2822:  
2823:  JASSUB 
2824:  
2825:  
2826:  The Fastest JavaScript SSA/ASS Subtitle Renderer For Browsers. 
2827:  
2828: JASSUB is a JS wrapper for libass, which renders SSA/ASS subtitles directly in your browser. It uses Emscripten to compile libass' C++ code to WASM, and WebGL for hardware acceleration. 
2829:  
2830:  
2831:  Demo 
2832:  
2833:  
2834: ## Features 
2835:  
2836: * Supports all SSA/ASS features (everything libass supports) 
2837: * Supports all OpenType, TrueType and WOFF fonts, as well as embedded fonts 
2838: * Supports anamorphic videos [(on browsers which support it)](https://caniuse.com/mdn-api_htmlvideoelement_requestvideoframecallback) 
2839: * Supports color space mangling [(on browsers which support it)](https://caniuse.com/mdn-api_videocolorspace) 
2840: * Capable of using local fonts [(on browsers which support it)](https://caniuse.com/mdn-api_window_querylocalfonts) 
2841: * Capable of finding fonts online (opt-in, done via Google Fonts API) 
2842: * Works fast (all the heavy lifting is done by WebAssembly and WebGL, with absolutely minimal JS glue) 
2843: * Is fully multi-threaded 
2844: * Is asynchronous (renders when available, not in order of execution) 
2845: * Benefits from hardware acceleration (uses WebGL) 
2846: * Doesn't manipulate the DOM to render subtitles 
2847: * Easy to use - just connect it to video element 
2848:  
2849: ## Requirements 
2850:  
2851: The 
2852:  
2853: ```json
2854: {
2855:   "Cross-Origin-Embedder-Policy": "require-corp",
2856:   "Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy": "same-origin"
2857: }
2858: ```
2859:  
2860: headers are recommended to use this library, as it uses SharedArrayBuffer for multi-threading, but if you can't set them, it will fallback automatically to work in single-threaded mode. Firefox doesn't support threading so they are not required there. 
2861:  
2862: At minimum WASM + TextDecoder + OffscreenCanvas + Web Workers + Proxy + Fetch + Promise + getVideoPlaybackQuality/requestVideoFrameCallback are required for JASSUB to work. 
2863:  
2864:   
2865:  
2866: JASSUB supports Chrome/Safari/Firefox 80/17/105, you bring the support down to 67/16.2/68 if you enable some flags/settings in your browser for these features. For other engines polyfills might be needed. Babel is also recommended if you need to support older JS engines as JASSUB ships as ES modules with modern syntax. 
2867:  
2868:  
2869:  
2870: ## Usage 
2871:  
2872: Install the library via: 
2873:  
2874: ```shell
2875: [p]npm i jassub
2876: ```
2877:  
2878: ```js
2879: import JASSUB from 'jassub'
2880: ...
2881: const instance = new JASSUB({
2882:   video: document.querySelector('video'),
2883:   subUrl: './tracks/sub.ass'
2884: })
2885: ```
2886:  
2887: If you use a custom bundler, and need to override the worker and wasm URLs you can instead do: 
2888:  
2889: ```js
2890: import JASSUB from 'jassub'
2891: import workerUrl from 'jassub/dist/jassub-worker.js?worker&url'
2892: import wasmUrl from 'jassub/dist/jassub-worker.wasm?url' // non-SIMD fallback
2893: import modernWasmUrl from 'jassub/dist/jassub-worker-modern.wasm?url' // SIMD
2894: ...
2895: const instance = new JASSUB({
2896:   video: document.querySelector('video'),
2897:   subContent: subtitleString,
2898:   workerUrl, // you can also use: `new URL('jassub/dist/jassub-worker.js', import.meta.url)` instead of importing it as an url, or whatever solution suits you
2899:   wasmUrl,
2900:   modernWasmUrl
2901: })
2902: ```
2903:  
2904: However this shoud almost never be necessary. 
2905:  
2906: ## Using only with canvas 
2907:  
2908: You're also able to use it without any video. However, that requires you to set the time the subtitles should render at yourself: 
2909:  
2910: ```js
2911: import JASSUB from 'jassub'
2912: ...
2913: const instance = new JASSUB({
2914:   canvas: document.querySelector('canvas'),
2915:   subUrl: './tracks/sub.ass'
2916: })
2917: ...
2918: await instance.ready
2919: 
2920: instance.manualRender({ expectedDisplayTime: performance.now(), width: 1920, height: 1080, mediaTime: 10.20 })
2921: ```
2922:  
2923: # Docs 
2924:  
2925: The library is fully typed, so you can simply browse the types of `instance` or `instance.renderer`. "Private" fields are prefixed with `_` such as `_fontId` or `_findAvailableFonts`, and shouldn't be used by developers, but can if the need arises. 
2926:  
2927: `instance.renderer` calls are ALWAYS async as it's a remote worker, which means you should always await/then them for the IPC call to be serialized!!! For example: 
2928:  
2929: ```ts
2930: const x = instance.renderer.useLocalFonts // does nothing, returns IPC proxy object
2931: const y = await instance.renderer.useLocalFonts // returns true/false
2932: 
2933: instance.renderer.useLocalFonts = false // this is fine
2934: await (instance.renderer.useLocalFonts = false) // or u can await it for safety
2935: 
2936: instance.renderer
2937: ...
2938: Font('Gandhi Sans') // this is
2939: ...
2940: , sets default font
2941: ...
2942: .setDefaultFont('Gandhi Sans') // or you can await if if you want
2943: ...
2944: ](https://github
2945: ...
2946: ). 
2947:

3008: ## About finding fonts online

3027: Title: altqx/akarisub
3028: URL: https://github.com/altqx/akarisub
3029: Published: N/A
3030: Author: N/A
3031: Highlights:
3032: ## README
3033: 
3034:  
3035:  AkariSub
3036:  
3037:  
3038:  JavaScript SSA/ASS Subtitle Renderer.
3039:  
3040: 
3041: > **Note:** This is a fork of [ThaUnknown's JASSUB](https://github.com/ThaUnknown/jassub) legacy version with hyper optimizations, intelligent caching, and many quality-of-life improvements.
3042: ...
3043: AkariSub is a JS wrapper for libass, which renders SSA/ASS subtitles directly in your browser. It uses Emscripten to compile libass' C++ code to WASM.
3044: ...
3045: - Supports most SSA/ASS features (everything libass supports)
3046: - Supports all OpenType, TrueType and WOFF fonts, as well as embedded fonts
3047: - Supports anamorphic videos [(on browsers which support it)](https://caniuse.com/mdn-api_htmlvideoelement_requestvideoframecallback)
3048: - Supports different video color spaces [(on browsers which support it)](https://caniuse.com/mdn-api_videocolorspace)
3049: - Capable of using local fonts [(on browsers which support it)](https://caniuse.com/mdn-api_window_querylocalfonts)
3050: - Works fast (all the heavy lifting is done by WebAssembly)
3051: - Is fully threaded (on browsers which support it, it's capable of working fully on a separate thread)
3052: - Is asynchronous (renders when available, not in order of execution)
3053: - Benefits from hardware acceleration (uses hardware accelerated canvas API's)
3054: - Doesn't manipulate the DOM to render subtitles
3055: - Easy to use - just connect it to video element
3056: ...
3057: - **GPU Rendering** - Hardware-accelerated rendering with an automatic fallback chain: WebGPU [(on browsers which support it)](https://caniuse.com/webgpu) → WebGL2 → Canvas2D
3058: - **Hyper Optimizations** - Performance improvements and intelligent caching for smoother playback
3059: - **Proper Fontconfig Implementation** - add Fontconfig support with multiple fallback fonts supported
3060: - **Encrypted Subtitles** - optionally load AES-GCM encrypted subtitle payloads that are decrypted inside the worker, so plaintext never touches the main thread
3061: - **Statistics Reporting** - Built-in statistics and performance metrics for debugging and monitoring
3062: - **TypeScript Support** - Full TypeScript definitions and type safety
3063: - **Updated Dependencies** - All dependencies updated to their latest versions, including libass
3064: ...
3065: ## Using only with canvas
3066: ...
3067: You're also able to use it without any video. However, that requires you to set the time the subtitles should render at yourself. Disable `onDemandRender` (it relies on video frame callbacks) and drive the clock manually:
3068: ...
3069: const renderer = new AkariSub({
3070:   canvas: document.querySelector('canvas'),
3071:   subUrl: './tracks/sub.ass',
3072:   onDemandRender: false
3073: })
3074: ...
3075: ## GPU Rendering
3076: ...
3077: AkariSub automatically picks the fastest available renderer: WebGPU → WebGL2 → Canvas2D. GPU renderers are used when no custom canvas is given and the browser supports them:
3078: ...
3079: The default options are best, and automatically fallback to the next fastest options in line, when the API's they use are unsupported. You can however forcefully change this behavior by specifying options.
3080: ...
3081: | Option | Type | Default | Description |
3082: |--------|------|---------|-------------|
3083: | `video` | HTMLVideoElement | - | Video to use as target for rendering and event listeners |
3084: | `canvas` | HTMLCanvasElement | - | Canvas to use for manual handling (optional if video is provided) |
3085: | `blendMode` | `'js'` \| `'wasm'` | `'wasm'` | Image blending mode. WASM is better for low-end devices, JS for hardware acceleration |
3086: | `asyncRender` | boolean | auto | Render via ImageBitmap. Defaults to `true` on Canvas2D paths and `false` when a GPU renderer is active (raw buffers upload with fewer copies) or on WebKit |
3087: | `offscreenRender` | boolean | `true` | Render fully on the worker, greatly reduces CPU usage |
3088: | `onDemandRender` | boolean | `true` | Render subtitles as the video player renders frames |
3089: | `targetFps` | number | `24` | Target FPS when not using onDemandRender |
3090: | `timeOffset` | number | `0` | Subtitle time offset in seconds |
3091: | `debug` | boolean | `false` | Enable debug logging |
3092: ...
3093: | `prescaleFactor` | number | `1.0` | Scale factor for subtitles canvas |
3094: | `prescaleHeightLimit` | number | `1080` | Height limit for prescaling in pixels |
3095: ...
3096: | `maxRenderHeight` | number | `0` | Maximum render height (0 = no limit) |
3097: ...
3098: | `workerUrl` | string | `'akarisub-worker.js'` | URL to the worker script |
3099: | `wasmUrl` | string | `'akarisub-worker.wasm'` | URL to the WASM binary |
3100: ...
3101: | `subUrl` | string | - | URL of the subtitle file to play |
3102: | `subContent` | string \| Uint8Array \| ArrayBuffer | - | Content of the subtitle file to play |
3103: | `encryptedSubContent` | EncryptedSubtitleContent | - | AES-GCM encrypted subtitle payload, decrypted inside the worker |
3104: ...
3105: | `fonts` | (string \| Uint8Array)[] | - | Array of font URLs or Uint8Arrays to force load |
3106: ...
3107: | `availableFonts` | Record<string, string \| Uint8Array> | `{'liberation sans': './default.woff2'}` | Available fonts map (lowercase name → URL/data) |
3108: | `fallbackFonts` | string[] | `['liberation sans']` | Fallback font families in order, used for the fontconfig cascade |
3109: ...
3110: | `useLocalFonts` | boolean | `true` | Use Local Font Access API if available |
3111: ...
3112: | `libassMemoryLimit` | number | `128` | libass bitmap cache memory limit in MiB |
3113: | `libassGlyphLimit` | number | `2048` | libass glyph cache limit |
3114: | `fullTrackWarmup` | boolean | `false` | Pre-render early track windows after load to warm libass caches |
3115: | `onCanvasFallback` | function | - | Callback when no GPU renderer is available (Canvas2D fallback) |
3116: ...

3203: ## What is SAB
3204: ...
3205: SABRE.js adds stylish subtitles to web videos.
3206: 
3207: ## What formats does SABRE.js support?
3208: ...
3209: SABRE.js is a full renderer for Substation Alpha Subtitles and Advanced Substation Alpha Subtitles.
3210: It allows you to draw styled/stylized subtitles over web video with multiple fonts, colors, animations and effects.
3211: 
3212:  [![karaoke demo loop](gallery/images/demo_loop.gif)](gallery/video/demo_loop.mp4) 
3213: 
3214: ## Gallery
3215: ...
3216: You can retrieve an instance of the library by calling `sabre.SABRERenderer()`.
3217: 
3218: Example:
3219: ...
3220: // Load the contents of the subtitle file.
3221: fetch("subtitles.ass").then((response) => response.arrayBuffer()).then((subs) => {
3222:     // Load the fonts using opentype.js and put them in the fonts array. (this function also returns a promise)
3223:     opentype.load("arial.ttf", (font) => {
3224:         fonts.push(font);
3225:         // Initialize the renderer
3226:         renderer = sabre.SABRERenderer({
3227:             fonts:fonts,
3228:             subtitles:subs,
3229:             colorSpace:sabre.VideoColorSpaces.AUTOMATIC,
3230:             resolution:[1280,720], // Display resolution of the video in CSS pixels.
3231:             nativeResolution:[1280,720] // Resolution of the video file in real pixels (only used if the color space is AUTOMATIC or AUTOMATIC_PC).
3232:         });
3233:         // Schedule your frame callback using either requestAnimationFrame or requestVideoFrameCallback
3234:     });
3235: });
3236: 
3237: ```

3271: You may then call `loadSubtitles` passing in an ArrayBuffer containing the contents of the subtitle file and set the
3272: viewport with `setViewport` as shown in the example above. Anytime the video or player is resized you should call
3273: `setViewport` with the new dimensions of the player.

3275: #### Functions
3276: 
3277:  
3278:  loadSubtitles(subtitles, fonts) ⇒ void 
3279:  Begins the process of parsing the passed subtitles in SSA/ASS format into subtitle events. 
3280:  
3281:  setColorSpace(colorSpace, [width], [height]) ⇒ void 
3282:  Configures the output colorspace to the set value (or guesses when automatic is specified based on resolution).
3283: ...
3284: Note: HDR support is stubbed and unimplemented currently. 
3285:  
3286:  setViewport(width, height) ⇒ void 
3287:  Updates the resolution (in CSS pixels) at which the subtitles are rendered (if the player is resized, for example). 
3288:  
3289:  checkReadyToRender() ⇒ boolean 
3290:  Checks if the renderer is ready to render a frame. 
3291:  
3292:  getFrame(time) ⇒ ImageBitmap 
3293:  Fetches a rendered frame of subtitles as an ImageBitmap, returns null if ImageBitmap is unsupported. 
3294:  
3295:  getFrameAsUri(time, callback) ⇒ void 
3296:  Fetches a rendered frame of subtitles as an object uri. 
3297:  
3298:  drawFrame(time, canvas, [contextType]) ⇒ void 
3299:  Fetches a rendered frame of subtitles to a canvas.

3328: Title: sweet-subtitle - npmx

3330: Published: N/A

3332: Highlights:
3333: Subtitle renderer and parser for web video: ASS/SSA, SRT, WebVTT, SBV, TTML/DFXP with React/Vue/Angular adapters and WASM effects
3334: ...
3335: Subtitle renderer and parser for web video, with full ASS/SSA support and multi-format parsing (SRT, WebVTT, SBV, TTML/DFXP), powered by Canvas 2D + Rust WASM.
3336: ...
3337: - Multi-format — SRT, WebVTT, ASS/SSA, SBV, TTML/DFXP
3338: - ASS advanced rendering — styles, positioning (`\pos`,`\move`,`\org`), rotation (`\frz/frx/fry`), animation (`\t` with accel), karaoke (`\k/\kf/\ko`), clip (`\clip/\iclip`), drawing commands (`\p`), fade (`\fad/\fade`), per-character alpha, border styles, etc.
3339: - Gaussian blur —`\blur` and`\be` rendered via Rust WASM (3-pass box blur), with CSS filter fallback
3340: - Text decorations — underline (`\u`), strikeout (`\s`)
3341: - Scale transforms — horizontal scale (`\fscx`), vertical scale (`\fscy`)
3342: - Auto text wrapping — long lines wrap within video bounds
3343: - Encoding detection — UTF-8, UTF-16LE/BE (with BOM), GBK auto-detection
3344: - Rust WASM bundled — WASM binary ships inside the package, zero extra config for users
3345: - Lightweight — zero runtime dependencies, tree-shakeable ESM/CJS dual output
3346: - Resilient parsing — tolerates malformed ASS files (wrong Format lines, missing PlayRes, case-insensitive sections, BOM, flexible timestamps)
3347: ...

3349: | --- | --- | --- |
3350: | SRT | Yes | Yes |
3351: | WebVTT | Yes | Yes |
3352: | ASS/SSA | Yes | Yes (advanced effects) |
3353: | SBV | Yes | Yes |
3354: | TTML/DFXP | Yes | Yes |

3441: ...
3442: However compared to WebAssembly solutions, it's almost impossible for DOM to render exactly same result in every pixels as VSFilter or libass, ASS.js will provide best efforts to accurate rendering.

3647: ...
3648: ```
3649: #!/bin/bash
3650: 
3651: # Image as video.
3652: # Usage: ./img4vid.sh image.png audio.aac 720
3653: # $1 = PNG/JPG      $2 = M4A/MP3        $3 = Height (px)
3654: 
3655: ffmpeg -i "$1" -i "$2" \
3656:     -loop 1 -vf "scale='min($3,iw)':-2,format=yuv420p" \
3657:     -c:v libx264 -preset medium -profile:v main \
3658:     -c:a aac -shortest -movflags +faststart \
3659:     output.mp4
3660: 
3661: ```

3674: Title: @ffmpeg.wasm/main

3678: Highlights:
3679: ffmpeg.wasm is a pure Webassembly port of FFmpeg. It enables video & audio record, convert and stream right inside browsers.
3680: ...
3681: `ffmpeg.wasm` provides simple to use APIs, to transcode a video you only need few lines of code:
3682: ...
3683: ```ts
3684: import { readFile, writeFile } from "fs/promises";
3685: import { FFmpeg } from "@ffmpeg.wasm/main";
3686: ...
3687: const ffmpeg = await FFmpeg.create({ core: "@ffmpeg.wasm/core-mt" });
3688: 
3689: ffmpeg.fs.writeFile("test.avi", await readFile("./test.avi"));
3690: await ffmpeg.run("-i", "test.avi", "test.mp4");
3691: await writeFile("./test.mp4", ffmpeg.fs.readFile("test.mp4"));
3692: process.exit(0);
3693: ```

3851: ...
3852: Audio codecs (decode and encode): AAC, MP3, Opus, Vorbis, FLAC, PCM, AC3.
3853: ...

3856: Frame extraction:`-vf "select=eq(n,N)"` to pull a specific frame, or`-vf "fps=1"` to extract one frame per second.
3857: ...
3858: Concat: the`concat` filter and demuxer both work for stitching clips together.
3859: ...

3861: ...

3863: ...
3864: PNG) |
3865: ...
3866: × rt |
3867: ...
3868: | Audio extract (AAC remux, no re-encode) | 0.3 s | 200× rt | Container repack only. |
3869: | Crop
3870: ...
3871: full-res because fewer pixels.
3872: ...
3873: When to use it: when you need per-frame control. WebCodecs is the right primitive if you're writing a real timeline editor with frame-accurate ins and outs and effects on individual frames. The tradeoff is that you have to assemble the container yourself — WebCodecs gives you`EncodedVideoChunk` objects, not an MP4 file. You either pipe them into a JavaScript MP4 muxer (mp4-muxer, mp4box.js) or back into ffmpeg.wasm's remuxer.
3874: ...
3875: WebCodecs
3876: ...
3877: "The encode is fine but the audio is missing." A common ffmpeg gotcha unrelated to wasm: if you specify`-c:v libx264` you've implicitly asked for video-only output. Add`-c:a aac -b:a 128k`(or`-c:a copy` if the input audio codec is already what you want).
3878: ...
3879: - Want to trim, transcode, or convert a video in the browser, without a server? ffmpeg.wasm.
3880: - Want frame-accurate per-frame manipulation with a custom timeline? WebCodecs (and use ffmpeg.wasm only for the final mux).
3881: - Want to capture a screen or webcam stream and save it?`MediaRecorder`, not ffmpeg.wasm. (Then ffmpeg.wasm for post-processing.)
3882: ```

3958:     u_scale: 0.6,
3959:     u_rotation: 0,
3960:     u_offsetX: 0,
3961:     u_offsetY: 0,
3962:     u_originX: 0.5,
3963:     u_originY: 0.5,
3964:   },
3965: });
3966: ...
3967: Pixel noise intensity, related to canvas and not scalable
3968: ...
3969: Curly-shaped noise intensity for paper fiber simulation
3970: ...
3971: Cell-based crumple pattern intensity
3972: ...
3973: Scale of the cell-based crumple pattern
3974: ...
3975: Depth of the paper folds
3976: ...
3977: Number of fold lines in the paper texture
3978: ...
3979: Random seed for folds, crumples, and dots pattern variation
3980: ...
3981: ### Animation Parameters
3982: ...
3983: Animation speed multiplier (0 for static)
3984: ...
3985: Manual frame control for animation
3986: ...
3987: The PaperTexture shader comes with several built-in presets:
3988: ...
3989: - Default: Classic paper texture with balanced settings
3990: - Cardboard: Brown cardboard appearance with fiber texture
3991: - Abstract: Colorful abstract paper with strong folds
3992: - Details: High-detail texture for close-up effects
3993: ...
3994: ### Cardboard Texture
3995: ...
3996: ```tsx
3997: <PaperTexture
3998:   colorFront="#c7b89e"
3999:   colorBack="#999180"
4000:   contrast={0.4}
4001:   fiber={0.35}
4002:   fiberSize={0.14}
4003:   crumples={0.7}
4004:   crumpleSize={0.1}
4005:   folds={0}
4006:   drops={0.1}
4007: />
4008: ...
4009: ### Abstract Paper
4010: ...
4011: ```tsx
4012: <PaperTexture
4013:   colorFront="#00eeff"
4014:   colorBack="#ff0a81"
4015:   contrast={0.85}
4016:   fiber={0.1}
4017:   folds={1}
4018:   foldCount={3}
4019:   drops={0.2}
4020:   seed={2.2}
4021: />
4022: ...
4023: ### Performance Notes
4024: ...
4025: - This shader is static by default (no animation)
4026: - Uses multiple noise layers for realistic texture
4027: - Requires a noise texture (automatically handled in React)
4028: - Best performance with moderate `foldCount` values (5-10)

4032: Title: p5.paper
4033: URL: https://registry.npmjs.org/p5.paper
4034: Published: 2026-03-08T17:36:10.000Z
4035: Author: Thai
4036: Highlights:
4037: A p5.js shader library for realistic paper textures, grain, color bleed, and blemishes.
4038: ...
4039: ---
4040: 
4041:  
4042:  
4043:  
4044:  A high-performance WebGL post-processing library for authentic paper textures in p5.js.
4045:  
4046: 
4047: [![npm version](https://img.shields.io/npm/v/p5.paper.svg)](https://www.npmjs.com/package/p5.paper)
4048:  [![License: MIT](https://img.shields.io/badge/License-MIT-yellow.svg)](https://opensource.org/licenses/MIT)
4049:  
4050: 
4051: ---
4052: ...
4053: A p5.js shader library for realistic paper textures, grain, color bleed, and blemishes.
4054: ...
4055: currently contains:
4056: ...
4057: - The core `p5Paper` post-processing class in `src/index.js`
4058: - GLSL shaders in `src/shaders/paper.vert` and `src/shaders/paper.frag`
4059: - A Vite-powered demo sketch using `main.js` and `index.html`
4060: ...
4061: For standard global p5.js projects, include the library in your ` ` right after p5.js. Because this is a post-processing effect, you must draw your artwork to an off-screen buffer (`createGraphics`) and then pass that buffer to `p5.paper`.
4062: ...
4063: >
4064: ...
4065: UTF-8">
4066: ...
4067: 1.9.0/p5.min.
4068: ...
4069: "></script>
4070:     <!-- 2. Load the p5.paper UMD file -->
4071: ...
4072: script src="./dist/p5.paper.umd.js"></script>
4073:   </head>
4074:   <body>
4075:     <script>
4076:       let paper;
4077:       let drawBuffer;
4078:       
4079:       function setup() {
4080:         createCanvas(800, 600);
4081:         
4082:         // 1. Create an off-screen buffer to draw your art
4083:         drawBuffer = createGraphics(width, height);
4084:         drawBuffer.background('#f4f1ea');
4085:         
4086:         // 2. Initialize the p5.paper library globally
4087:         paper = new p5Paper(width, height);
4088:       }
4089: 
4090:       function draw() {
4091:         // 3. Draw your artwork onto the buffer (NOT the main canvas)
4092:         if (mouseIsPressed) {
4093:           drawBuffer.noStroke();
4094:           drawBuffer.fill(20, 25, 30, 50);
4095:           drawBuffer.circle(mouseX, mouseY, 20);
4096:         }
4097: 
4098:         // 4. Define your paper texture parameters
4099:         const params = {
4100:             tex: 0.15,      // Procedural grooves (0 - 0.5)
4101:             grit: 0.30,     // Blemishes/dirt (0 - 1.0)
4102:             grain: 0.12,    // Micro grain (0 - 0.5)
4103:             vignette: 0.60, // Edge darkening (0 - 1.5)
4104:             bleed: 0.003    // Color bleed/chromatic effect (0 - 0.02)
4105:         };
4106: 
4107:         // 5. Apply the shader effect to the buffer and draw to the main screen
4108:         let finalImage = paper.apply(drawBuffer, params);
4109:         image(finalImage, 0, 0);
4110:       }
4111:     </script>
4112:   </body>
4113: </html>
4114: ...
4115: p5.paper exposes the following post-processing effects, controlled via the `params` object passed to `paper.apply`:
4116: ...
4117: - **`tex` – Procedural grooves**: Simulated paper fibers / grooves.
4118: - **`grit` – Grittiness / blemishes**: Dirt, specks, and surface imperfections.
4119: - **`grain` – Micro grain**: Fine-grain noise over the image.
4120: - **`vignette` – Vignette**: Edge darkening from the center outward.
4121: - **`bleed` – Color bleed**: Chromatic/color bleed effect on edges.
4122: - **`imgTex` – Image texture intensity**: Strength of the custom seamless image map.
4123: - **`imgScale` – Image tiling scale**: Tiling scale of the custom image map.
4124: ...
4125: [p5.brush](https://github.com/acamposuribe/p5.brush) is a natural drawing library for p5.js — pencils, charcoal, markers, watercolor fills, and hatch patterns. The two libraries complement each other naturally: p5.brush produces the artwork, p5.paper makes it feel like it was drawn on physical paper.
4126: ...
4127: Because p5.brush requires a **WEBGL canvas**, and p5.paper applies its shader pass to a separate buffer, you need one extra surface — a WEBGL `p5.Graphics` buffer — that sits between the two:
4128: ...
4129: ```
4130: p5.Graphics buffer (WEBGL)
4131:   └── brush draws into it via brush.load(pg)
4132:         └── paper.apply(pg, params) reads it as a texture
4133:               └── p.image(result, 0, 0) displays the composited output
4134: ...
4135: const sketch = (p) => {
4136:   let pg;    // WEBGL graphics buffer — brush draws here
4137:   let paper; // p5Paper reads from pg, outputs shader result
4138: 
4139:   p.setup = () => {
4140:     // Tell p5.brush which p5 instance to use
4141:     brush.instance(p);
4142: 
4143:     // Main canvas must be WEBGL — required by p5.brush
4144:     p.createCanvas(W, H, p.WEBGL);
4145:     p.imageMode(p.CORNER);
4146:     p.angleMode(p.DEGREES);
4147: 
4148:     // Off-screen WEBGL buffer that brush will draw into
4149:     // p5.paper will read this as its source texture
4150:     pg = p.createGraphics(W, H, p.WEBGL);
4151:     pg.angleMode(p.DEGREES);
4152:     pg.background('#f4f1ea'); // paper base color
4153: 
4154:     // Initialize p5.paper — pass p so it uses instance-mode createGraphics
4155:     paper = new p5Paper(W, H, p);
4156: 
4157:     // Point brush at the offscreen buffer and draw your scene
4158:     brush.load(pg);
4159:     brush.scaleBrushes(4);
4160: 
4161:     // CRITICAL: pg is a WEBGL buffer — its origin is the CENTER, not the
4162:     // top-left. Without this translate, all your coordinates are measured
4163:     // from the middle of the buffer and shapes appear off-screen or clipped.
4164:     pg.push();
4165:     pg.translate(-W / 2, -H / 2);
4166: 
4167:     brush.noField();
4168:     brush.noClip();
4169: 
4170:     p.randomSeed(42);
4171:     p.noiseSeed(42);
4172:     brush.set('HB', '#21313f', 1);
4173:     brush.noFill();
4174:     brush.line(60, 80, 420, 180);
4175: 
4176:     p.randomSeed(43);
4177:     p.noiseSeed(43);
4178:     brush.noStroke();
4179:     brush.fill('#174d71', 90);
4180:     brush.circle(200, 320, 80);
4181: 
4182:     pg.pop(); // restores the WEBGL origin
4183: 
4184:     // Restore brush to the main canvas when done
4185:     brush.load();
4186: 
4187:     p.noLoop();
4188:   };
4189: 
4190:   p.draw = () => {
4191:     // WEBGL origin is center — shift to top-left coordinates
4192:     p.translate(-W / 2, -H / 2);
4193: 
4194:     // Apply paper texture shader and draw to screen
4195:     const result = paper.apply(pg, {
4196:       tex:      0.15,  // procedural paper grooves    (0 – 0.5)
4197:       grit:     0.30,  // blemishes / dirt            (0 – 1.0)
4198:       grain:    0.12,  // micro grain noise           (0 – 0.5)
4199:       vignette: 0.60,  // edge darkening              (0 – 1.5)
4200:       bleed:    0.003, // chromatic color bleed       (0 – 0.02)
4201:     });
4202:     p.image(result, 0, 0);
4203:   };
4204: };
4205: ...
4206: - **`pg.push()` / `pg.translate(-W / 2, -H / 2)` / `pg.pop()` wraps all brush drawing** — this is the most common gotcha. `pg` is a WEBGL buffer whose origin sits at the center, not the top-left. Every coordinate you pass to `brush.line()`, `brush.rect()`, `brush.circle()` etc. is measured from that center. Without this translate, shapes that appear to be at `(W *
4207: ...
4208: 0.7, H * 0.6)` are actually off-screen, and only strokes that start near `(0, 0)` happen to land near the visible center. Wrapping with `pg.push() / pg
4209: ...
4210: translate(-W/2, -H/2) / pg.pop()` makes all coordinates behave as expected top-left relative.
4211: ...
4212: be WEBGL** — p
4213: ...
4214: .brush's blending system requires a WebGL2 context. Passing a 2D graphics buffer will throw.
4215: ...
4216: - **`brush.scaleBrushes()` should match your canvas size** — the default brush sizes are calibrated for small canvases. At 700×500 use `scaleBrushes(2)`, at 1200×1200 use `scaleBrushes(4)`. Too small a scale and strokes render as invisible hairlines.
4217: ...
4218: - **`new p5Paper(W, H, p)`** — the third argument tells p5.paper to use `p.createGraphics` instead of the global. This is required in instance mode and harmless in global mode.
4219: ...
4220: - **`p.translate(-W / 2, -H / 2)` at the top of `draw()`** — the main canvas is also WEBGL, so `p.image(result, 0, 0)` needs the same origin shift or the output renders offset from the top-left corner.
4221:

4310: Title: Vanilla JavaScript Usage - Paper Shaders
4311: URL: https://paper-design-shaders.mintlify.app/guides/vanilla-usage
4312: Published: N/A
4313: Author: N/A
4314: Highlights:
4315: # Vanilla JavaScript Usage
4316: ...
4317: > Complete guide to using Paper Shaders with vanilla JavaScript
4318: ...
4319: package from npm
4320: ...
4321: ```bash
4322: npm install @paper-design/shaders
4323: ...
4324: The vanilla package provides the `ShaderMount` class for mounting shaders to DOM elements:
4325: ...
4326: ```javascript
4327: import {
4328:   ShaderMount,
4329:   meshGradientFragmentShader,
4330:   getShaderColorFromString
4331: } from '@paper-design/shaders';
4332: ...
4333: // Mount the shader
4334: const shaderMount = new ShaderMount(
4335:   container,
4336:   meshGradientFragmentShader,
4337:   uniforms,
4338:   undefined, // webGlContextAttributes
4339:   1,         // speed (1 = normal, 0 = static)
4340:   0          // starting frame
4341: );
4342: ...
4343: The container element will automatically get a `data-paper-shader` attribute and
4344: the shader canvas will be positioned absolutely to fill it.
4345: ...
4346: ## ShaderMount Constructor
4347: ...
4348: The `ShaderMount` class signature:
4349: ...
4350: ```typescript
4351: new ShaderMount(
4352:   parentElement: HTMLElement,
4353:   fragmentShader: string,
4354:   uniforms: ShaderMountUniforms,
4355:   webGlContextAttributes?: WebGLContextAttributes,
4356:   speed?: number,              // Default: 0
4357:   frame?: number,              // Default: 0
4358:   minPixelRatio?: number,      // Default: 2
4359:   maxPixelCount?: number,      // Default: 1920 * 1080 * 4
4360:   mipmaps?: string[]           // Default: []
4361: )
4362: ...
4363: - parentElement: The DOM element to mount the shader canvas into
4364: - fragmentShader: The GLSL fragment shader code (import from package)
4365: - uniforms: Object containing uniform values for the shader
4366: - webGlContextAttributes: Optional WebGL context configuration
4367: - speed: Animation speed multiplier (0 = static, negative = reverse)
4368: - frame: Starting animation frame in milliseconds
4369: - minPixelRatio: Minimum pixel ratio for rendering (default: 2)
4370: - maxPixelCount: Maximum pixels to render (default: 1920 × 1080 × 4)
4371: - mipmaps: Array of uniform names that should use mipmaps (e.g., `['u_image']`)
4372: ...
4373: ## Available Shaders
4374: ...
4375: Import fragment shaders from the package:
4376: ...
4377: ```javascript
4378: import {
4379:   // Gradients
4380:   meshGradientFragmentShader,
4381:   staticMeshGradientFragmentShader,
4382:   staticRadialGradientFragmentShader,
4383:   grainGradientFragmentShader,
4384:   
4385:   // Patterns
4386:   dotOrbitFragmentShader,
4387:   dotGridFragmentShader,
4388:   voronoiFragmentShader,
4389:   wavesFragmentShader,
4390:   
4391:   // Noise & Particles
4392:   simplexNoiseFragmentShader,
4393:   perlinNoiseFragmentShader,
4394:   neuroNoiseFragmentShader,
4395:   metaballsFragmentShader,
4396:   smokeRingFragmentShader,
4397:   
4398:   // Animations
4399:   swirlFragmentShader,
4400:   spiralFragmentShader,
4401:   warpFragmentShader,
4402:   godRaysFragmentShader,
4403:   pulsingBorderFragmentShader,
4404:   colorPanelsFragmentShader,
4405:   
4406:   // Image Filters
4407:   waterFragmentShader,
4408:   flutedGlassFragmentShader,
4409:   imageDitheringFragmentShader,
4410:   heatmapFragmentShader,
4411:   liquidMetalFragmentShader,
4412:   halftoneDotsFragmentShader,
4413:   halftoneCmykFragmentShader,
4414:   
4415:   // Textures
4416:   paperTextureFragmentShader,
4417:   ditheringFragmentShader,
4418: } from '@paper-design/shaders';
4419: 
4420: ```
4421: ...
4422: ## Working with Images
4423: ...
4424: For image-based shaders, pass loaded `HTMLImageElement` objects:
4425: ...
4426: ## Animation Control
4427: ...
4428: ### Setting Speed
4429: ...
4430: ```javascript
4431: // Start/change animation speed
4432: shaderMount.setSpeed(1);    // Normal speed
4433: shaderMount.setSpeed(0);    // Pause (stops requestAnimationFrame)
4434: shaderMount.setSpeed(0.5);  // Slow motion
4435: shaderMount.setSpeed(-1);   // Reverse
4436: 
4437: ```
4438: ...
4439: ## Performance Control
4440: ...
4441: ## Complete Example
4442: ...
4443: #shader-container
4444: ...
4445: <body>
4446:   <div id="shader-container"></div>
4447:   <button id="toggle">Pause/Play</button>
4448:   <button id="change-colors">Change Colors</button>
4449:   
4450:   <script type="module">
4451:     import {
4452:       ShaderMount,
4453:       meshGradientFragmentShader,
4454:       getShaderColorFromString,
4455:     } from '@paper-design/shaders';
4456:     
4457:     const container = document.getElementById('shader-container');
4458:     
4459:     const uniforms = {
4460:       u_colors: [
4461:         getShaderColorFromString('#5100ff'),
4462:         getShaderColorFromString('#00ff80'),
4463:         getShaderColorFromString('#ffcc00'),
4464:         getShaderColorFromString('#ea00ff'),
4465:       ],
4466:       u_colorsCount: 4,
4467:       u_distortion: 0.8,
4468:       u_swirl: 0.1,
4469:       u_grainMixer: 0,
4470:       u_grainOverlay: 0,
4471:       u_fit: 1,
4472:       u_scale: 1,
4473:       u_rotation: 0,
4474:       u_offsetX: 0,
4475:       u_offsetY: 0,
4476:       u_originX: 0.5,
4477:       u_originY: 0.5,
4478:       u_worldWidth: 0,
4479:       u_worldHeight: 0,
4480:     };
4481:     
4482:     const shaderMount = new ShaderMount(
4483:       container,
4484:       meshGradientFragmentShader,
4485:       uniforms,
4486:       undefined,
4487:       1, // speed
4488:       0  // frame
4489:     );
4490:     
4491:     // Toggle animation
4492:     let isPlaying = true;
4493:     document.getElementById('toggle').addEventListener('click', () => {
4494:       isPlaying = !isPlaying;
4495:       shaderMount.setSpeed(isPlaying ? 1 : 0);
4496:     });
4497:     
4498:     // Change colors
4499:     document.getElementById('change-colors').addEventListener('click', () => {
4500:       shaderMount.setUniforms({
4501:         u_colors: [
4502:           getShaderColorFromString('#ff0000'),
4503:           getShaderColorFromString('#00ff00'),
4504:           getShaderColorFromString('#0000ff'),
4505:           getShaderColorFromString('#ffff00'),
4506:         ],
4507:       });
4508:     });
4509:     
4510:     // Clean up on page unload
4511:     window.addEventListener('beforeunload', () => {
4512: ...
4513: shaderMount.dispose();
4514:     });
4515:   </script>
4516: </body>
4517: </html>
4518: ...
4519: ## Fit Options

4525: const gl = canvas.getContext('webgl2');

4837: Key point: Canvas 2D is the reason those premium libs (paper shaders, ASS renderers, bloom/glow via glfx) don't integrate. Canvas 2D is CPU-bound, no real shaders, no GPU blur, limited filters (browser ctx.filter is okay but @napi-rs/canvas lacks it / getImageData after filter breaks).
4838: 
4839: But Canvas 2D has huge advantages: determinism, easy Node export via @napi-rs/canvas, simple API, no GPU needed on server, frame-accurate, small footprint.
4840: 
4841: Alternatives:
4842: - WebGL (PixiJS / Three.js / OGL / regl): GPU, shaders, premium effects (bloom, paper, particles, chromatic aberration), fast. Determinism harder (float precision, async). Bigger bundle (Three.js ~600KB). But PixiJS is designed for 2D + has filters (including @pixi/filter-bloom, displacement, etc.) — actually PixiJS has a rich filter ecosystem (pixi-filters) which includes bloom, glow, shockwave, CRT, etc. That's a strong candidate for premium 2D with GPU.
4843: - WebGPU: future, best perf, but Node support limited and browser support still maturing.
4844: - SVG: vector, crisp text, easy styling, can use CSS. Node export via resvg or sharp. But no real-time GPU effects, harder for complex particle/blur, and animation is declarative. Good for crisp vector graphics but not "motion graphics" heavy effects.
4845: - Hybrid DOM/HTML+CSS captured via headless browser (Playwright/Puppeteer): lets you use ALL web tech (CSS animations, WebGL, SVG, ASS renderers, paper shaders). Pros: maximum compatibility with premium libs, easy to get premium look. Cons: NOT deterministic frame-accurate easily (timing in browser), heavy (runs a browser), slow, and @napi-rs/canvas won't help. This is basically what Remotion does but Remotion is React. Actually many "premium" tools render via headless Chrome and screenshot frames. Determinism is achievable if you drive time manually.

4866: 
4867: Good question — and yes, Canvas 2D is exactly why every premium library we found (paper shaders, ASS/karaoke renderers, glfx bloom) doesn't plug in cleanly. Here's the honest picture.
4868: 
4869: ## Why Canvas was chosen (from your own README)
4870: > "Deterministic Canvas rendering for browser preview and native export."
4871: 
4872: Two reasons drove it:
4873: 1. **Determinism** — same code, same frame every run (important for LLM-generated videos you can lint/re-render).
4874: 2. **Native Node export** — `@napi-rs/canvas` gives you a Canvas2D context in Node with *no browser*, so you render frames server/lambda-side and feed ffmpeg.
4875: 
4876: ## Canvas 2D — pros / cons
4877: **Pros**
4878: - Zero GPU dependency; runs in Node, CI, Lambda, edge.
4879: - Tiny footprint, simple API, easy to make frame-accurate.
4880: - `@napi-rs/canvas` parity means browser + Node share one renderer.
4881: 
4882: **Cons**
4883: - **CPU-bound** — blur/particles/glow are slow at 1080p/4K.
4884: - **No real shaders** — bloom, chromatic aberration, paper, displacement are either impossible or hacked via `getImageData` (slow, and `@napi-rs/canvas` breaks `getImageData` after `ctx.filter`).
4885: - **This is your bottleneck**: every "premium" lib we found is WebGL/DOM, not Canvas2D.
4886: 
4887: ## Better alternatives
4888: 
4889: | Approach | Pros | Cons |
4890: |---|---|---|
4891: | **WebGL via PixiJS** (2D GPU + `pixi-filters`: bloom, glow, CRT, displacement, shockwave) | GPU speed; rich *existing* filter ecosystem (premium look, no build-from-scratch); Canvas-like API; works in browser | Node export needs `headless-gl` (flaky) or browser capture; bigger bundle (~400KB) |
4892: | **WebGL via OGL/regl** (lightweight) | Tiny, fast, full shader control (paper/bloom trivial) | You write the effect code; smaller community than Pixi |
4893: | **WebGPU** | Best perf, compute shaders | Immature Node support; too early for a lib you ship |
4894: | **SVG + resvg/sharp** (Node) | Crisp vector text/charts, easy CSS styling, small | No GPU effects, weak for particles/blur; not true motion-graphics |
4895: | **Headless-browser capture** (Playwright/Puppeteer → ffmpeg) | Uses *all* web tech (CSS, WebGL, ASS renderers, paper shaders); easiest path to premium | Not deterministic-by-default; heavy (full browser); slower; needs Chromium on server |
4896: | **Hybrid: Canvas2D core + optional WebGL/Pixi enhanced layer** | Keeps determinism + Node export; upgrades browser preview & effects | Two render paths to maintain |
4897: 
4898: ## Recommendation
4899: Keep **Canvas 2D as the deterministic/Node core** (it's the right call for server export and LLM reproducibility), but add an **optional WebGL "enhanced renderer"** for the premium layer:
4900: 
4901: - **Browser preview + effects**: PixiJS + `pixi-filters` (bloom, glow, chromatic aberration, displacement/paper) — all existing libs, no from-scratch.
4902: - **Node export**: keep Canvas2D path as-is; for GPU effects in export, either (a) render in a headless browser and capture frames, or (b) accept that heavy GPU effects are browser/preview-only and bake lighter 2D equivalents for the offline path.
4903: 
4904: That resolves the "every library needs WebGL" problem without throwing away your determinism/Node advantage.
4905:

5006: Bonuses:

5011: - Deterministic? WebGL is less deterministic than Canvas2D due to float precision, driver differences, async. But if you fix a GL backend and seed, it's mostly reproducible. Determinism across machines is weaker.

5019: - **Determinism regression**: driver/GPU differences, float precision, async readback. LLM-generated videos that need reproducible frames suffer. You can mitigate by fixing backend but not guaranteed cross-machine.

5021: - **Text rendering**: WebGL text needs glyph atlases; complex text layout (RTL, CJK, wrapping, karaoke per-word) is more work than Canvas2D's built-in text. You often render text to a canvas texture anyway → so you still depend on Canvas2D for text rasterization internally, then upload as texture. So "replace canvas entirely" is partly illusory for text/captions.
5022: - **@napi-rs/canvas removal**: you lose the clean Node path.
5023: - **Complexity**: shader management, context loss handling, WebGL state.
5024: - **Accessibility/compat**: older devices, Safari quirks.
5025: - **getImageData/readback** for effects that need pixel data (caption burn-in? no). Some pipelines need readback which is slow on GPU.

5038: 
5039: Going all-in on WebGL (e.g. PixiJS/OGL as the sole renderer) flips the tradeoff completely. Here's the honest ledger.
5040: 
5041: ## Bonuses
5042: - **Premium effects become trivial** — bloom, glow, chromatic aberration, displacement, paper fold/crumble, fluid, particles, lens flare, glitch, CRT are just shaders/filters. You reuse `pixi-filters`, `postprocessing`, SABRE.js (WebGL ASS), `@paper-design/shaders` directly → satisfies "use existing libs, don't build from scratch."
5043: - **Real GPU performance** — 4K/60fps, thousands of particles, native blur (no CPU `stackblur`), cheap multi-plane parallax via depth.
5044: - **Cleaner effect pipeline** — post-FX as a filter chain, not `getImageData` hacks.
5045: - **All the libraries we found now integrate** (they're WebGL/DOM, not Canvas2D).
5046: 
5047: ## Tradeoffs
5048: - **Server/Node export breaks.** There is no native WebGL in Node. Your options:
5049:   - `headless-gl` (`gl`) — only **WebGL1**, flaky on Lambda, needs system GL libs. Can't run WebGL2 shaders like `@paper-design/shaders`.
5050:   - **Headless browser (Playwright)** — this *is* a browser server-side; you lose the lightweight native-export advantage entirely.
5051:   - → This kills your current headline feature: deterministic native export via `@napi-rs/canvas`.
5052: - **Determinism regresses.** GPU float precision, driver differences, and async readback make frames less reproducible across machines — bad for LLM-generated videos you lint/re-render.
5053: - **You never truly escape Canvas2D.** WebGL text is rasterized to a glyph atlas *using Canvas2D*, then uploaded as a texture. So captions/karaoke still secretly depend on canvas; you just stop drawing with it directly.
5054: - **Bigger bundle** — PixiJS ~400KB+, vs your "don't load a lot on network" goal (mitigated by tree-shaking, but real).
5055: - **More moving parts** — context-loss handling, shader state, Safari/old-device quirks, slower pixel readback for any CPU post-step.
5056: 
5057: ## Bottom line
5058: | If premium visuals is now #1 priority | If determinism + lightweight Node export is still #1 priority |
5059: |---|---|
5060: | Go all-WebGL + move export to a **headless-browser capture** pipeline (Playwright → ffmpeg) | Keep Canvas2D core, add WebGL as an **enhanced layer** |
5061: 
5062: All-WebGL is the right call *only if* you're willing to drop the native Node export story and accept weaker cross-machine determinism. Given your repo's stated identity ("deterministic… native export… LLM-friendly"), I'd still recommend the **hybrid**: Canvas2D stays the reproducible core/Node path, WebGL becomes the premium browser layer.

5233: 2. **Headless Browser**: Executes the React code to render frames using Puppeteer/Chrome, managed via `internalOpenBrowser` [packages/renderer/src/open-browser.ts112-122](https://github.com/remotion-dev/remotion/blob/4ac84726/packages/renderer/src/open-browser.ts#L112-L122)

5235: These components are orchestrated through the `RemotionServer` abstraction, which manages their lifecycle and coordinates communication between them [packages/renderer/src/prepare-server.ts23-30](https://github.com/remotion-dev/remotion/blob/4ac84726/packages/renderer/src/prepare-server.ts#L23-L30)
5236: ...
5237: The server handles two distinct types of requests:
5238: ...
5239: 1. **Proxy requests (`/proxy`)**: Forwarded to OffthreadVideo server for video frame extraction [packages/renderer/src/serve-static.ts45-47](https://github.com/remotion-dev/remotion/blob/4ac84726/packages/renderer/src/serve-static.ts#L45-L47)
5240: 2. **Bundle requests**: Static file serving for the bundle via `serveHandler` [packages/renderer/src/serve-static.ts55-56](https://github.com/remotion-dev/remotion/blob/4ac84726/packages/renderer/src/serve-static.ts#L55-L56
5241: ...
5242: ## OffthreadVideo Server
5243: ...
5244: The OffthreadVideo server provides a proxy endpoint for extracting video frames at specific timestamps, used by the `<OffthreadVideo>` component during rendering.
5245: ...
5246: ### Frame Extraction Process
5247: ...
5248: The proxy server coordinates video frame extraction through several steps:
5249: ...
5250: 1. **Download**: Asset is downloaded and cached via `downloadAsset()` [packages/renderer/src/offthread-video-server.ts129-138](https://github.com/remotion-dev/remotion/blob/4ac84726/packages/renderer/src/offthread-video-server.ts#L129-L138)
5251: 2. **Extract**: Compositor executes `ExtractFrame` command [packages/renderer/src/offthread-video-server.ts147-154](https://github.com/remotion-dev/remotion/blob/4ac84726/packages/renderer/src/offthread-video-server.ts#L147-L154)
5252: ...
5253: 3. **Format Detection**: First bytes identify PNG (`0x89 0x50 0x4E`) or BMP (`0x42 0x4D`) [packages/renderer/src/offthread-video-server.ts184-192](https://github
5254: ...
5255: com/remotion-dev/remotion/blob/4ac84726/packages/renderer/src/offthread-video-server.ts#L184-L192
5256: ...
5257: ## Browser Management
5258: ...
5259: Remotion uses Puppeteer to launch and control headless Chromium instances. The browser lifecycle is managed through several abstractions:
5260: ...
5261: ### Browser Fetching and Installation
5262: ...
5263: Remotion includes a built-in browser fetcher to ensure a compatible Chromium version is available.
5264: ...
5265: * **Download Logic**: `downloadBrowser` determines the platform (mac-arm64, linux64, etc.) and fetches the corresponding "Chrome for Testing" or "Headless Shell" binary [packages/renderer/src/browser/BrowserFetcher.ts108-122](https://github.com/remotion-dev/remotion/blob/4ac84726/packages/renderer/src/browser/BrowserFetcher.ts#L108-L122)
5266: ...
5267: **Extraction**: Downloads are unzipped and the executable path is resolved based on the `chromeMode` [packages/renderer/src/browser/BrowserFetcher.ts183-215](https://github.com/remotion-dev/remotion/blob/4ac84726/packages/renderer/src/browser/BrowserFetcher.ts#L183-L215)
5268: ...
5269: ### Environment Injection
5270: ...
5271: Before rendering, the browser page must be initialized with props and environment variables. This ensures the React application has access to the correct state and configuration during frame extraction.
5272: ...
5273: 1. **setPropsAndEnv**: This function navigates the Puppeteer `Page` to the `serveUrl`. It uses `page.evaluateOnNewDocument` to inject configuration into the global `window` object before any script runs [packages/renderer/src/set-props-and-env.ts61-92](https://github.com/remotion-dev/remotion/blob/4ac84726/packages/renderer/src/set-props-and-env.ts#L61-L92)
5274: ...
5275: 2. **Global Variables**: Injected variables include `window.remotion\_inputProps`, `window.remotion\_envVariables`, `window.remotion\_proxyPort` (for the OffthreadVideo server), and `window.remotion\_puppeteerTimeout` [packages/renderer/src/set-props-and-env.ts108-158](https://github.com/remotion-dev/remotion/blob/4ac84726/packages/renderer/src/set-props-and-env.ts#L108-L158)
5276: ...
5277: **Bundle Mode**: To
5278: ...
5279: page is switched to `evaluation` mode via `window.remotion\_setBundleMode` [packages/renderer/src/get-compositions.ts95-105](https://github.com/remotion-dev/remotion/blob/4ac84726/packages/renderer/src/get-compositions.ts#L95-L105)
5280: ...
5281: 4. **Ready Signal**: The renderer waits for the application to signal it is ready by checking `window.remotion\_renderReady` (via `waitForReady`) before attempting to seek to frames [packages/renderer/src/get-compositions.ts107-113](https://github.com/remotion-dev/remotion/blob/4ac84726/packages/renderer/src/get-compositions.ts#L107-L113) [packages/core/src/index.ts41](https://github.com/remotion-dev/remotion/blob/4ac84726/packages/core/src/index.ts#L41-L41)
5282: ...
5283: ### Browser Options
5284: ...
5285: |`headless`|`boolean`|Toggles headless mode (`--headless=new` or `--headless=old`) [packages/renderer/src/open-browser.ts208-212](https://github.com/remotion-dev/remotion/blob/4ac84726/packages/renderer/src/open-browser.ts#L208-L212)|
5286: ...
5287: |`video-threads`|`number`|Sets Chromium's `--video-threads` flag for decoding [packages/renderer/src/open-browser.ts201](https://github.com/remotion-dev/remotion/blob/4ac84726/packages/renderer/src/open-browser.ts#L201-L201)|

5291: Title: How client-side rendering works | Remotion | Make videos programmatically
5292: URL: https://www.remotion.dev/docs/client-side-rendering/how-it-works
5293: Published: 2026-06-16T20:49:28.000Z
5294: Author: N/A
5295: Highlights:
5296: The biggest challenge of client-side rendering is that it is not possible to capture the browser viewport.
5297: ...
5298: Only certain HTML elements such as `, `, `or` can be captured natively.
5299: ...
5300: Unlike in server-side rendering, where a pixel-perfect screenshot is made, in client-side rendering Remotion places all elements on a canvas based on how it believes they are positioned and appearing in the DOM.
5301: ...
5302: For this, Remotion has developed a sophisticated algorithm for calculating the placement of the elements on the canvas.
5303: ...
5304: Of course, we cannot support all web features, so only a specific subset of elements and styles are supported.
5305: ...
5306: ## Rendering process
5307: ...
5308: First, the component is mounted in the DOM in a place where it is not visible to the user.
5309: ...
5310: Simultaneously, an empty canvas is initialized.
5311: ...
5312: ### Frame capture process
5313: ...
5314: For each frame that needs to be rendered, the renderer uses `element.createTreeWalker()` to find all elements and text nodes in the DOM. Nodes that have `display: none` and their children are skipped.
5315: ...
5316: For each capturable element, the renderer:
5317: ...
5318: 1. Goes up the DOM tree and resets all `transform` CSS properties to `none`.
5319: 2. Gets the bounding box using `.getBoundingClientRect()`, as well as the bounding boxes of the parent elements.
5320: 3. Adds up the transforms and positions to determine the original placement of the element in the DOM.
5321: 4. Gets the pixels of the element - for `, `, `` elements, those can be captured. For text nodes, the layout is reconstructed manually.
5322: 5. Draws them to the canvas according to the calculated placement.
5323: ...
5324: ### Audio capture
5325: ...
5326: Audio from mounted `](/docs/media/audio) and [` elements is captured and mixed together, and added to the audio track of the video.
5327: ...
5328: Mediabunny is used to encode the frames and processed audio into a video file.
5329: ...
5330: ## Capturing pixels
5331: ...
5332: For `, `, `` elements, the pixels can be captured natively using the widely documented techniques.
5333: ...
5334: For rendering other types of elements, only a subset of properties are supported such as `background`, `border` and `border-radius`. These styles are drawn to the canvas manually with the Canvas 2D API.
5335: ...
5336: ### Capturing text nodes
5337: ...
5338: For text nodes, more layout calculations need to be made.
5339: ...
5340: Normally, it is not possible to get the bounding box of a text node, but by wrapping a text node in a `` element, we can call `.getBoundingClientRect()` on the span to get the bounding box and resolve the transforms as described above.
5341: ...
5342: Then `Intl.Segmenter` is used to split the text into words, and each token is again wrapped in a ``. For each token, `.getBoundingClientRect()` is called and the tokens are drawn to the canvas.
5343: ...
5344: In the end, the DOM is reset to its original state.
5345: ...
5346: ## Context isolation
5347: ...
5348: Renders happen in the same browser environment as your app. This means CSS and Tailwind variables will automatically work, but you run the risk of conflicts with the host page.
5349: ...
5350: See Limitations for more details to ensure your code works with client-side rendering.
5351:

12353: 57:     maxX = Math.max(maxX, x + w / 2)

12355: 59:     maxY = Math.max(maxY, y + h / 2)

12375: 79:

12377: 81:     const maxH = Math.max(...sizes.map(([, h]) => h), 0)

1: /**
2:  * Captions — SRT ingest + karaoke / word timings using staggered delays.

5: import type { EntranceAnimation } from './types'

20: /** Minimal SRT parser (supports basic blocks). */
21: export function parseSrt(input: string): CaptionCue[] {
22:   const cues: CaptionCue[] = []
23:   const blocks = input.replace(/\r\n/g, '\n').trim().split(/\n\s*\n/)
24:   for (const block of blocks) {
25:     const lines = block.trim().split('\n').filter(Boolean)
26:     if (lines.length < 2) continue
27:     let timeLineIdx = 0
28:     if (/^\d+$/.test(lines[0].trim())) timeLineIdx = 1
29:     const timeLine = lines[timeLineIdx]
30:     const m = timeLine.match(/(\d{2}:\d{2}:\d{2}[,.]\d{3})\s*-->\s*(\d{2}:\d{2}:\d{2}[,.]\d{3})/)
31:     if (!m) continue
32:     const textLines = lines.slice(timeLineIdx + 1).join(' ').trim()
33:     cues.push({
34:       start: parseSrtTs(m[1]),
35:       end: parseSrtTs(m[2]),
36:       text: textLines,
37:     })
38:   }
39:   return cues.sort((a, b) => a.start - b.start)
40: }
41: 
42: function parseSrtTs(s: string): number {
43:   const norm = s.replace(',', '.')
44:   const [hh, mm, rest] = norm.split(':')
45:   const ss = Number.parseFloat(rest)
46:   const h = Number(hh)
47:   const m = Number(mm)
48:   return ((h * 60 + m) * 60) + ss
49: }
50: 
51: export function splitWords(s: string): string[] {
52:   return s.trim().split(/\s+/).filter(Boolean)
53: }
54: 
55: export type CaptionStyle =
56:   | 'plain'
57:   | 'pill'
58:   | 'karaoke'
59:   | 'wordPop'
60:   | 'highlightKeywords'
61:   | 'slam'
62:   | 'clipWipe'
63:   | 'weightShift'
64: 
65: /**
66:  * Produce per-line/per-word timings for karaoke from a caption cue inside a scene.
67:  * `cueStartScene` — seconds between scene start and this cue start.
68:  */
69: export function buildCaptionWordSpans(
70:   fullText: string,
71:   cueDuration: number,
72:   style: CaptionStyle,
73: ): Array<{ word: string; delay: number; emphasize?: boolean }> {
74:   const words = splitWords(fullText)
75:   const step =
76:     cueDuration > 0
77:       ? Math.max(0.08, cueDuration / Math.max(words.length, 1))
78:       : Math.max(0.12, 1.8 / Math.max(words.length, 1))
79: 
80:   return words.map((word, index) => {
81:     const emphasize = style === 'highlightKeywords' && /^[#A-Za-z0-9]/.test(word) && word.length <= 22
82:     return { word: style === 'wordPop' ? word.toUpperCase() : word, delay: index * step, emphasize }
83:   })
84: }
85: 
86: export function pickCaptionEntrance(style: CaptionStyle): EntranceAnimation {
87:   switch (style) {
88:     case 'wordPop':
89:     case 'slam':
90:       return 'tactileIn'
91:     case 'clipWipe':
92:       return 'revealLeft'
93:     case 'karaoke':
94:     case 'highlightKeywords':
95:       return 'slideUp'
96:     case 'weightShift':
97:       return 'fadeIn'
98:     default:
99:       return 'fadeIn'
100:   }
101: }
102: 
103: export function karaokeBackgroundForWord(style: CaptionStyle): string | undefined {
104:   if (style === 'pill') return 'rgba(255,255,255,0.16)'
105:   if (style === 'karaoke' || style === 'highlightKeywords') return 'rgba(255,255,255,0.08)'
106:   return undefined
107: }

          c.textAlign = 'left'
        }

          cx += c.measureText(ch).width + letterSpacing
        }
        c.restore()
      } else {
        c.fillText(line, anchorX, lineY)
      }
